-
-
-
RA-ERD-C13-2 / Reflect, take a step back, assess its actions and know how to question itself (C13: Discern and refine one's desires, identify
one's value system, and take responsibility for one's professional, personal and societal choices, in order to give meaning to one's actions.)
AA-ERD-C13-2-01 / To develop reflective thinking about one's own learning activity, and to identify one's tastes with a projective aim / p (Bp.1)
AA-ERD-C13-2-02 / Give feedback, take a step back, self-evaluate one's actions and know how to question oneself. (B2.3)
-
-
RA-MEO-C08-4 / Identify, within a culture, the factors necessary for a work environment adapted to the challenges of the organisation (in all its
complexity) (C08: Manage teams with a view to collective success, respecting the common good. Pay attention to health and safety issues.
Mobilize your ability to influence and position yourself accordingly in your professional environment.)
AA-MEO-C08-4-02 / Engage in effective teamwork to produce deliverables within the timeframe required (Bp.2)
-
-
RA-PROJET-C02-1 / Identify the different fields of knowledge involved in projects, products and processes (C02: Organize theoretical and
experimental knowledge to identify and link the various components of projects, and interact effectively with all those involved.)
AA-PROJET-C02-1-01 / Identify the expertise needed for the project (B1.2)

Ghinwa EL HAJJ SLEIMAN, Icam Ouest - Campus of Nantes - ghinwa.el-hajj-sleiman@icam.fr
Tutor's guide
Code du PBL : O 1.1 EEM PBL5
Nom du PBL : Statiques des fluides
Titre du PBL : Chavirage de deux bateaux
Énoncé de la situation-problème
Deux bateaux (différents) de la compagnie HMS transportent la même cargaison. L'un coule lors de son chargement au port, l'autre pendant une tempête.
La société s’adresse à des experts pour identifier les causes de ce chavirage.
Un expert estime que le naufrage est dû à une mauvaise appréciation des notions de gîte, roulis, tangage, déplacement et du tirant d’eau tandis qu’un
autre souligne le tonnage, la répartition du chargement, la calaison et la lège.
Votre bureau d’étude est également sollicité pour clarifier cette situation, notamment en termes de flottabilité et stabilité navale.
DESCRIPTION

(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1152

=== PAGE 204 ===
DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1773
Sequence in brief
Bloc 1 : faire un travail de recherche documentaire suivi d’un portrait vidéo sur deux personnages célèbres de nationalité étrangère
Travail en binôme ou trinôme
L’exercice consiste à confronter deux groupes dont l’axe de présentation de chaque personne génère une opposition
Il appartient à l’enseignant, lors du choix de ces personnes, de valider ou non la proposition des étudiants pour la pertinence de l’exercice
L’enseignant choisit les paires qui vont s'affronter
Chaque intervenant peut suggérer une liste de personnages hispanophones ou germanophones
L’étudiant rédigera en cours une note d'observation structurée d’une demi-page pour rendre compte de ses choix et de son cheminement par rapport au
personnage (pourquoi ai-je choisi ce personnage, quelles ont été mes motivations, qu’ai-je appris le concernant, qu’ai-je découvert…)
L’étudiant, lors de ses recherches, variera les supports
Suggestion de situations :
Incarnation de deux personnages et imagination d’une situation conflictuelle fictive entre ces deux personnages
Exemples :
• Gandhi vs. Martin Luther King
• Steve Jobs vs. Bill Gates
• Tolkien vs. George Martin
• Dali vs. Picasso
Bloc 2 : travail de renforcement de la langue en lien avec le projet ou non (renforcement grammatical, renforcement lexical, compréhension et expression
orales) en adéquation avec le niveau de la classe.
Un test de niveau sera fait en début de semestre.
Une préparation à une certification externe pourra être proposée à ceux qui le souhaitent en vue d’un départ à l’étranger ou ceux qui veulent valider un
niveau.
Le programme de cette préparation est évolutif dans la progression de OP à O2.
SKILLS (LEARNING OBJECTIVES AND LEARNING GOALS)
Icam International Bachelor's
-
-

using engineering tools and methods and digital agility.)

-
-
RA-HUM-C06-1 / Find relevant and reliable information, be able to verify their relevance with respect to a given topic (C06: Search for and extract
relevant information from a variety of qualified sources. Use critical thinking skills to adapt information to the problem at hand.)
AA-HUM-C06-1-01 / Apply appropriate methods of bibliographical research in order to extract and reformulate relevant information (B1.2)
-
-
RA-HUM-C06-3 / Select and quantify the sources of information, verify their relevance with respect to a given topic by assessing the authority of
the source. (C06: Search for and extract relevant information from a variety of qualified sources. Use critical thinking skills to adapt information to
the problem at hand.)
AA-HUM-C06-3-01 / Adapt to and confront situations using one's critical thinking and ability to persuade (B3.6)

-

AA-HUM-C06-3-01 / Adapt to and confront situations using one's critical thinking and ability to persuade (B3.6)
-
-
-
-
RA-HUM-C12-1 / Communicate both in writing and orally in a foreign language (C12: Exercise one's profession as an engineer, in both English
and French, in an intercultural situation, thanks to one's knowledge of cultural systems and experiences.)
AA-HUM-C12-1-01 / Communicate orally with ease and spontaneity, using appropriate vocabulary and correct grammar (B3.6)
AA-HUM-C12-1-02 / Write with appropriate vocabulary while respecting the grammatical and structural rules of the language (B3.6)
AA-HUM-C12-1-03 / Manage a situation and express one's expectations (logic, imagination & creativity) with clarity, conviction and precision in
a professional context in a foreign language (B2.3)
ASSIGNMENTS & ASSESSMENTS
CRITERIAS
AA-HUM-C04-1-01 - Create an appropriate support for a presentation (poster, slides, video...)-
-
CRIT_0001-NM - Produce supporting material without any spelling mistakes (During Activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0263-NM - Choose the right support corresponding to the desired type of communication (During Activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0264-NM - Present a concise support that is easy to read (key words, embedded elements, subtitles, etc.) (During Activity,
When spotted)
AA-HUM-C06-3-01 - Adapt to and confront situations using one's critical thinking and ability to persuade-
-
CRIT_0282-NM - Can understand straightforward factual information on everyday and non-day topics, recognising general messages
and points of detail (During Activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0283-NM - Can understand the main points of clear standard input on familiar matters regularly encountered in work, including
short stories. (During Activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0284-NM - Can develop a simple argument so that it is understood most of the time (During Activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0285-NM - Can give brief reasons and explanations for opinions and facts. (During Activity, When spotted)
-
AA-HUM-C12-1-01 - Communicate orally with ease and spontaneity, using appropriate vocabulary and correct grammar-
-
CRIT_0311-NM - Can exchange point of views and opinions in a simple but clear manner on topics that may or may not be common
or familiar to him/her (During Activity, When spotted)
AA-HUM-C12-1-02 - Write with appropriate vocabulary while respecting the grammatical and structural rules of the language-
-
CRIT_0003-NM - Can provide information on concrete topics, understand the information, ask questions about a problem or explain
the problem in general terms. (CEFR level B1) (During Activity, When spotted)
AA-HUM-C12-1-03 - Manage a situation and express one's expectations (logic, imagination & creativity) with clarity, conviction
and precision in a professional context in a foreign language
-
CRIT_0004-NM - Can understand direct factual information on everyday topics, recognising general messages, provided the structure
is clear and the accent common. (During Activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0005-NM - Can read direct factual texts on subjects related to his/her background and interests with a reasonable level of
comprehension (CEFR level B1). (During Activity, When spotted)

DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1147
Sequence in brief
This course introduces essential mathematical concepts, providing a solid foundation for further study and application in various fields.
1. Sets of Numbers: Understand the classification of numbers, including natural numbers, integers, rational numbers, real numbers, and complex
numbers, and explore their properties and relationships.
2. Functions (Injection, Surjection, Bijection): Study different types of functions—injective (one-to-one), surjective (onto), and bijective (both one-to-
one and onto)—and their significance in mapping and mathematical reasoning.
3. Complex Numbers: Explore the properties of complex numbers, their representation on the complex plane, and operations such as addition,
multiplication, and finding conjugates.
4. Algebraic Calculus: Focus on Algebraic Identities and the process of factorizing algebraic expressions, which are essential for simplifying and
solving algebraic equations.
5. Numerical Sequences: Delve into arithmetic and geometric sequences, examining their formulas, properties, and how to calculate the terms of
each sequence.
6. Statistics: Gain a solid understanding of key statistical concepts such as measures of central tendency (mean, median, mode) and dispersion
(variance, standard deviation), along with data analysis techniques.
7. Probability: Learn fundamental concepts of probability, including the use of tree diagrams for event visualization, binomial distribution for
discrete probability calculations, and prediction intervals for estimating outcomes within a specified range.
This course equips students with a thorough understanding of essential mathematical concepts, along with problem-solving techniques that apply to real-
world scenarios.
SKILLS (LEARNING OBJECTIVES AND LEARNING GOALS)
Icam International Bachelor's
-
-
-

monitor and carry out complex, multidisciplinary projects.)

AA-MIA-C01-1-05 / Handle complex numbers (B1.2)

-

needed to monitor and carry out complex, multidisciplinary projects.)

DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1146
Sequence in brief
various fields.
This module introduces advanced mathematical concepts, with a focus on functions and vector geometry, preparing students to apply these ideas in
1. Properties of Functions: Explore key properties such as the domain of definition, symmetry in function graphs, and variations (increasing,
decreasing behavior) to understand how functions behave and change.
2. Common Functions: Study frequently used functions like linear, quadratic, polynomial, exponential, logarithmic, and trigonometric, focusing on their
graphs and applications.
3. Derivatives of Functions: Learn to compute derivatives to analyze rates of change, curve slopes, and solve optimization problems.
4. Vector Geometry: Gain an understanding of vector representation in two and three dimensions, and how to perform operations such as addition,
subtraction, and magnitude calculation.
5. Projection: Study the concept of vector projection, a key tool for solving problems in physics, engineering, and applied mathematics.
6. Scalar Product (Dot Product): Understand the scalar product of vectors and its use in determining angles between vectors and projecting vectors
onto each other.

Sequence in brief
In this module, students will delve into advanced topics in calculus and mathematical analysis. They will explore integral calculus and its applications,
learning how to compute integrals using various techniques and apply them to solve real-world problems such as calculating the center of gravity or the
work done by a force.
The module also covers advanced function operations, including the differentiation of functions, calculating limits, and interpreting asymptotes and
parabolic branches. Students will work with partial derivatives, solve indeterminate forms, and utilize operators like gradient, divergence, and curl to
analyze functions of two or three variables.
This course provides a strong foundation in both integral and differential calculus, equipping students with the tools to solve complex mathematical
problems across a variety of applications.

DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):

Sequence in brief
In this advanced mathematics course, students will cover essential topics including:
• Differential Equations: Learn to formulate and solve various types of differential equations, applying techniques to analyze dynamic systems and
model real-world phenomena.
• Double and Triple Integrals: Explore the evaluation of multi-dimensional integrals, enabling students to compute areas and volumes in higher
dimensions.
• Laplace Transforms: Understand the use of Laplace transforms in simplifying the analysis of linear time-invariant systems, particularly in solving
ordinary differential equations.
• Series and Fourier Series: Study series representations of functions, focusing on Fourier series to express periodic functions as sums of sine and
cosine terms, facilitating applications in signal processing and analysis.
This course provides a solid foundation in advanced mathematical concepts, preparing students for applications in engineering, physics, and other
technical fields.

Sequence in brief
In this course, students will delve into the world of linear algebra, focusing on key concepts such as linear systems and matrices. They will explore how to
solve systems of linear equations, perform various matrix operations—including calculating determinants and inverses—and understand the relationships
between different variables within those systems. Additionally, students will learn to manipulate complex numbers, engaging with operations involving
these numbers while applying polar and exponential forms and utilizing De Moivre's theorem.
Through this comprehensive approach, students will gain valuable insights into the relevance of linear algebra in real-world scenarios, reinforcing their
mathematical foundation and preparing them for more advanced topics in mathematics and related fields.

DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1522
Sequence in brief
This course will equip students with knowledge and skills in control theory for continuous linear systems. Through both theoretical learning and practical
application, students will learn to model, analyze, and design simple controllers for automated systems. By the end of the course, they will be able to
implement control strategies using mathematical models and transfer functions to ensure system stability and desired performance.
Key Topics Covered:
• General concepts of control systems, including open and closed loop systems, automatic functions, and control system components.
• Mathematical modeling of systems, focusing on the use of Laplace transforms and transfer functions.
• Control system analysis, emphasizing transient and steady-state behavior, and graphical tools such as Bode diagrams and Nyquist plots.
• Hands-on system analysis using Scilab, where students will model, simulate, and design controllers.
• The design of controllers and compensators, particularly PID controllers, using both theoretical approaches and empirical methods.
This course is organized into both lecture sessions and practical work. Students will be introduced to the theoretical foundations of control systems and
will immediately apply this knowledge in practical sessions, working with real-world systems such as DC motors and airflow regulation systems. The
combination of theory and practice prepares students to design and implement control systems in various automation scenarios.
SKILLS (LEARNING OBJECTIVES AND LEARNING GOALS)

Sequence in brief

Sequence in brief
visualization.
This MOOC focuses on building essential spreadsheet skills, allowing students to manipulate data efficiently and apply key functions for data analysis and
1. Basic Spreadsheet Usage: Learn how to navigate and effectively use a spreadsheet for organizing and analyzing data.
2. Writing Formulas: Master the ability to write formulas and assign operations to cells, automating calculations and data processing.
3. Conditional Formatting: Understand how to apply conditional formatting to highlight important data based on specific criteria.
4. Creating Graphs: Develop the skills to create various types of charts and graphs, including bar charts, XY scatter plots, and pie charts, to visually
represent data.
5. Using Standard Functions: Learn to use common spreadsheet functions such as SUM, AVERAGE, and PRODUCT SUM to perform basic
mathematical operations.
6. Applying Dropdown Menus: Gain proficiency in applying dropdown menus to streamline data entry and improve spreadsheet interactivity.
7. Using Nested Functions: Explore the use of nested functions, combining multiple operations within a single formula for more complex calculations.
8. Conditional Functions: Apply conditional functions, such as IF, to automate decision-making processes based on data conditions.
9. Data Representation in Graphs: Learn how to represent data through graphs, making it easier to analyze trends and patterns visually.
This MOOC equips students with practical spreadsheet skills essential for data analysis, visualization, and problem-solving across various professional
contexts.

=== PAGE 318 ===
DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1142
Sequence in brief
This MOOC focuses on developing essential computational thinking skills, enabling students to solve problems using algorithms and basic programming
concepts.
1. Problem Solving with an Algorithmic Approach: Learn how to break down and solve simple problems using logical, step-by-step solutions.
2. Using Basic Operations and Variables: Master how to perform basic operations, such as assignments and mathematical calculations, while
choosing the correct variable types for different data.
3. Working with Loops and Conditions: Learn how to use loops (for, while) and conditional statements (if, else) to control the flow of a program,
including more complex cases involving nested loops and conditions.
4. User Interaction: Understand how to interact with users through input and output functions, making programs responsive to user inputs.
5. Creating Flowcharts: Visualize algorithms and their logic by creating flowcharts that illustrate the sequence and structure of the solution.
This MOOC equips students with the foundational skills needed to think computationally and solve problems efficiently using algorithmic techniques.
SKILLS (LEARNING OBJECTIVES AND LEARNING GOALS)
Icam International Bachelor's
-
-
-
RA-MIA-C03-1 / Implement all or part of a simple digital system and/or a simple automatic system (C03: Draw up specifications to characterize a
problem, and design solutions, systems and products, using practical and critical thinking skills.)
AA-MIA-C03-1-01 / Solving a simple problem with an algorithmic mind (Bp.2)
AA-MIA-C03-1-03 / Programming with a computer language in an imperative paradigm (B1.1)

Sequence in brief
In this Problem-Based Learning activity, students will explore the fundamentals of using a database, focusing on essential skills for effective data
management. They will learn how to insert and modify data, perform queries to extract information, and apply functions to analyze data. Additionally,
students will understand the importance of connecting different tables to relate data and use filters to refine their queries.

DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1516
Sequence in brief
In this Problem-Based Learning activity, students will explore how to improve the application process for students switching between Icam campuses.
Currently, this process relies on various manual methods that can be time-consuming and inefficient. Students will collaborate to create a solution that
simplifies and streamlines communication and information exchange.
Throughout the project, they will utilize their web development skills to build a dynamic web application. This will involve using HTML and CSS for the user
interface, along with a database for effective data management. By incorporating Python with a framework like Flask, students will connect the front-end
and back-end, enhancing functionality and user experience. This project will provide them with valuable experience in web application development and
database integration.

MIA_PBL12 - Maths - Operational Research

DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1524
Sequence in brief
In this Problem-Based Learning (PBL) activity, students will delve into operational research, emphasizing the application of numerical and mathematical
tools for decision-making. The focus will be on various optimization problems, leveraging techniques such as linear programming, integer programming,
constraint programming, and heuristics.
Through practical examples, students will learn to model linear programming problems, apply appropriate resolution methods, and represent data
graphically and in matrix form. They will also calculate the environmental cost of solutions and identify key variables, constraints, and objectives within
optimization scenarios.
This activity aims to enhance students' analytical skills and provide them with the necessary tools to tackle complex decision-making challenges
effectively.
Icam 18/08/2026 MIA_PBL12

(Moodle):

Sequence in brief
In this problem-based learning activity, students will reinforce their computational thinking by developing a program to solve a real-world challenge. The
scenario involves a broken drone, and the last payload retrieved contains a corrupted file. Students are tasked with analyzing and reconstructing an image
from the corrupted data.
Python will be the primary tool used for this activity, where students will write and implement code to reconstruct the image, strengthening their
programming and problem-solving skills in the process.

Sequence in brief
In this problem-based learning activity, students will advance their Python programming skills by stepping into the role of a game developer. Their task is
to creatively add new features to an existing game while enhancing their ability to program a graphical interface. This hands-on experience encourages
both technical and creative thinking, pushing students to design and implement game improvements.
The activity culminates in a competitive challenge, where students will test their skills against other campuses, providing an exciting and practical
conclusion to the learning experience.

Sequence in brief

encapsulation, and polymorphism—to enhance an existing game. They will design new classes, such as adding a new animal and game features like
fences, and ensure these new objects integrate seamlessly with the game's current mechanics. By extending functionality, students will practice using
OOP to structure code efficiently, allowing for easier modifications and reuse. This activity emphasizes hands-on experience with OOP principles,
focusing on how abstraction and class hierarchies can be leveraged to build more complex, maintainable systems.

Sequence in brief
In this Problem-Based Learning (PBL) activity, students will engage in UML modeling, building upon concepts introduced in earlier courses. The focus will
be on using UML to define and design systems effectively.
Students will learn to model both dynamic systems with sequence diagrams and static systems with class diagrams. They will also explore use case
diagrams to illustrate system functionality and gain a deeper understanding of class interactions and inheritance.
This activity aims to formalize the application of object-oriented concepts in computer science, enhancing students' ability to differentiate between
attributes and methods, implement inheritance, and clearly define system interactions.

DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1144
Sequence in brief
In this problem-based learning activity, students will address an issue with the crane's lift system. When the speed setpoint is adjusted using a joystick
controller, the container accelerates too rapidly at first and then slows down unexpectedly. Students will investigate the problem and implement a solution
to ensure a smoother acceleration that meets the required specifications.
Throughout this project, students will apply integral calculus and Laplace transforms to analyze the system dynamics and understand the principles of
dynamic control. They will model the crane's lift system as a linear time-invariant system, utilizing transfer functions and block diagrams. Additionally,
students will analyze the system's temporal response and performance characteristics, focusing on stability, speed, and accuracy. They will also use
Scilab/Matlab for modeling, simulating, and analyzing the automated system, along with tuning the regulator to achieve the desired performance. This
hands-on experience will deepen their understanding of dynamic systems and enhance their problem-solving skills.
SKILLS (LEARNING OBJECTIVES AND LEARNING GOALS)

(Moodle):

Sequence in brief
In this problem-based learning activity, students embark on an adventure to a mysterious island while learning about CSS and HTML as they develop
static web pages. The story unfolds as the crew, in search of treasures, encounters two peculiar characters: Hachtayaimail (representing HTML) and
Ceyaissaiys (representing CSS). Although their languages appear very different, the two characters communicate seamlessly, hinting at a deeper
connection.
Students will take on the role of helpers, bridging the gap between HTML and CSS by learning to create and style web content effectively. Through this
engaging narrative, they will gain practical experience in web development, mastering the fundamental concepts of structuring a webpage with HTML and
enhancing its appearance with CSS. This activity fosters creativity and problem-solving as students navigate the unique challenges presented by the
island's inhabitants while developing their skills in web design.

Sequence in brief
In this PBL, students will engage with the ball and beam system, a classic control problem that simulates real-world challenges like aircraft roll control.
They will explore control strategies, including manual, proportional, and proportional-derivative controls, to stabilize the system and maintain performance.
Through modeling, simulation, and analysis, students will learn to design and implement controllers, evaluate system responses, and tackle disturbances.
This hands-on experience will develop their understanding of dynamic systems and prepare them for complex engineering tasks in fields such as
automation and avionics.

Sequence in brief

algorithm and ensure their drone simulation operates smoothly from start to finish.

Sequence in brief
In this semester 2 project, students will develop a mobile application to guide a drone, with a special focus on implementing a "follow me" feature. Through
this project, students will learn to send HTTP requests to a web server, enabling real-time interaction between the app and the drone.
This hands-on activity combines mobile app development with web-based communication, providing students with valuable experience in both
programming and drone control technology.

Sequence in brief
In this project, students will embark on an exciting journey to build a boat autopilot using low-cost components, fostering hands-on learning through
experimentation. They will design and assemble various electronic components, integrating them to create a functional autopilot system for a boat.
Through this practical approach, students will gain valuable insights into the intersection of electronics and programming while developing their creativity
and problem-solving skills.
Throughout the project, students will develop programming skills while exploring fundamental concepts of electronics. They will learn to use libraries to
streamline their code and implement techniques for managing tasks and reading sensor data. This hands-on experience allows them to understand how
to make the autopilot system responsive to its environment. Overall, the project fosters creativity and encourages innovative thinking in the realm of
autonomous systems.

MIA_PP4 - MIA Integration
Programs: Icam International Bachelor's
Type: Project
Periods: B1.2
Campuses: Domains Language:
Icam Ouest - Campus of Nantes, Icam - Campus of Toulouse, Icam - Campus of Grand
Paris Sud, Icam - Campus of Lille
MIA / Mathematics, IT Engineering and Automatic Control Management Approach
, PROJ / Project
Home country
FTF Hours face to face: 5
STD Student workload: 3
TLH Total hours: 8
Pedagogical Coordinator:
Teacher/Animator:
Linda EFUELEFACK, Icam - Campus of Douala - linda.efuelefack@ucac-icam.com
Mohamed ABADI, Icam - Campus of Toulouse - mohamed.abadi@icam.fr
Rafael ALELUIA PORTO, Icam - Campus of Grand Paris Sud - rafael.aleluia@icam.fr
Romain DAILLY, Icam Ouest - Campus of Nantes - romain.dailly@icam.fr
Bedouen KANKEU, Icam - Campus of Douala - bedouen.kankeu@ucac-icam.com
Nicolas MELLIER, Icam - Campus of Grand Paris Sud - nicolas.mellier@icam.fr
Aloys NGUEPI NGUEGUIM, Icam - Campus of Douala - aloys.nguepi@ucac-icam.com
Diego ORDONEZ, Icam - Campus of Quito - diego.ordonez@icam.fr
Michel PHAN, Icam - Campus of Lille - michel.phan@icam.fr
DESCRIPTION
Course website
(Moodle):
https://moodle.icam.fr/course/view.php?id=1545
Sequence in brief
In this project, students will delve into automatic control systems to manage a boat's speed and direction effectively. They will gain theoretical knowledge
while exploring the principles of dynamic system control. The focus will be on understanding the concepts of system feedback, representing dynamic
systems through block diagrams, and synthesizing basic controllers like PID (Proportional-Integral-Derivative) controllers.
Students will implement these controllers in a practical context, adapting actuator behavior based on sensor inputs. They will also model and simulate
dynamic systems to analyze their responses, stability, speed, and precision. This project aims to equip students with essential skills in automation and
control system design, providing a solid foundation for future applications in the field of Internet of Things (IoT) devices.
Icam 18/08/2026 MIA_PP4

ASSIGNMENTS & ASSESSMENTS
CRITERIAS
AA-MME-C01-1-05 - Sizing a beam in statics with the different dimensioning parameters-
-
CRIT_SC_0001 - Calculate the mechanical stresses applied to a part in relation to the mechanical properties of materials (During
activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_SC_0026 - Identify the influence of section shape on the mechanical behavior of a beam (During activity, When spotted)
AA-MME-C01-1-06 - Identify the simple stresses of a beam and the mechanical rules that result from them-
-
CRIT_SC_0002 - Identify simple stresses on a beam (tension, compression, bending, shear, torsion) (During activity, When spotted)
AA-MME-C03-1-01 - Identify the mechanical parameters influencing the choice of materials and geometry-
-
CRIT_0732 - Choose a material according to its mechanical parameters (During activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0733 - Identify the different mechanical parameters related to materials (During activity, When spotted)
AA-MME-C03-1-11 - Measuring mechanical elements-
-
CRIT_0756 - Determine stresses in a beam using strain gage measurements (During activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0757 - Choose on simple systems a means of measurement of a displacement adapted (amplitude, precision,...), and by
making sure that the values presented respect the units in vigour. (During activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0758 - Choose on simple systems a relevant measurement system for rotational velocity (amplitude, precision,...), and by
making sure that the values presented use relevant units. (During activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0759 - Choose on simple systems a means of measurement of a force adapted (amplitude, precision,...), and by making sure
that the values presented respect the units in vigour. (During activity, When spotted)
AA-PROJET-C05-1-01 - Establish a problem and formulate hypotheses/choices-
-
CRIT_0928 - Establish one or more hypotheses that will lead to a choice (During activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0929 - Reformulate a problem (based on an observation, a question, etc.) (During activity, When spotted)
-
CRIT_0930 - Describe the experimental protocol for testing the hypotheses (During activity, When spotted)
